`CHANGELOG.md`:

```md
# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file. This project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/); see [docs/MIGRATION.md](docs/MIGRATION.md#version-policy) for the versioning policy.

## [0.1.0] - Unreleased

Initial implementation of the enterprise security SDK.

### Added

- Full TypeScript-first public API: `Security.scan()`, per-category checks (`isRooted`, `isJailbroken`, `isEmulator`, `isDeveloperModeEnabled`, `isUsbDebuggingEnabled`, `isDebuggerAttached`, `isMockLocationEnabled`, `isVpnConnected`, `isProxyEnabled`, `hasOverlayPermission`, `isScreenRecording`, `isScreenCaptured`, `isHookDetected`, `isFridaDetected`, `isXposedDetected`, `isLSPosedDetected`, `isTampered`, `getAppSignatureHash`, `verifyAppSignature`, `verifyApkIntegrity`, `isSslPinningSupported`).
- Configurable, JS-side device risk engine (`configureRiskEngine`/`resetRiskEngine`) producing `LOW`/`MEDIUM`/`HIGH`/`CRITICAL` classifications.
- Real-time event system (`addListener`/`removeListener`/`removeAllListeners`) covering `ROOT_DETECTED`, `JAILBREAK_DETECTED`, `VPN_CONNECTED`, `PROXY_ENABLED`, `SCREENSHOT`, `SCREEN_RECORDING`, `HOOK_DETECTED`, `DEBUGGER`, `TAMPER`, `SIGNATURE_INVALID`.
- SSL pinning helper utilities (`buildCertificatePin`, `isValidPinFormat`, `isValidPinSet`, `hasExpiredPins`).
- Full Android (Kotlin) native implementation: root/Magisk/BusyBox detection, emulator detection, Frida/Xposed/LSPosed/Substrate/Riru hook detection, developer-mode/USB-debugging/mock-location/debugger detection, VPN/proxy monitoring, screen recording/screenshot monitoring, overlay exposure, signature/APK integrity, tamper heuristics.
- Full iOS (Swift) native implementation: jailbreak detection, Simulator detection, Frida hook detection, debugger/mock-location detection, VPN/proxy monitoring, screen recording/screenshot monitoring (via `UIScreen.isCaptured` and screenshot notifications), signature-fingerprint/tamper heuristics.
- Enterprise dashboard example app (security score, per-check grid, live event log, refresh button).
- Full documentation set: Installation, API Reference, Examples, Migration Guide, Security Notes, FAQ, Troubleshooting, Best Practices.

### Notes

- Built on [Nitro Modules](https://nitro.margelo.com/) (New Architecture only) rather than hand-written TurboModules — see [README Architecture](README.md#architecture) for rationale.
- See [Security Notes](docs/SECURITY_NOTES.md#verification-status-of-this-implementation) for what has and hasn't been empirically validated pre-release.

```

`CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md`:

```md

# Contributor Covenant Code of Conduct

## Our Pledge

We as members, contributors, and leaders pledge to make participation in our
community a harassment-free experience for everyone, regardless of age, body
size, visible or invisible disability, ethnicity, sex characteristics, gender
identity and expression, level of experience, education, socio-economic status,
nationality, personal appearance, race, caste, color, religion, or sexual
identity and orientation.

We pledge to act and interact in ways that contribute to an open, welcoming,
diverse, inclusive, and healthy community.

## Our Standards

Examples of behavior that contributes to a positive environment for our
community include:

* Demonstrating empathy and kindness toward other people
* Being respectful of differing opinions, viewpoints, and experiences
* Giving and gracefully accepting constructive feedback
* Accepting responsibility and apologizing to those affected by our mistakes,
  and learning from the experience
* Focusing on what is best not just for us as individuals, but for the overall
  community

Examples of unacceptable behavior include:

* The use of sexualized language or imagery, and sexual attention or advances of
  any kind
* Trolling, insulting or derogatory comments, and personal or political attacks
* Public or private harassment
* Publishing others' private information, such as a physical or email address,
  without their explicit permission
* Other conduct which could reasonably be considered inappropriate in a
  professional setting

## Enforcement Responsibilities

Community leaders are responsible for clarifying and enforcing our standards of
acceptable behavior and will take appropriate and fair corrective action in
response to any behavior that they deem inappropriate, threatening, offensive,
or harmful.

Community leaders have the right and responsibility to remove, edit, or reject
comments, commits, code, wiki edits, issues, and other contributions that are
not aligned to this Code of Conduct, and will communicate reasons for moderation
decisions when appropriate.

## Scope

This Code of Conduct applies within all community spaces, and also applies when
an individual is officially representing the community in public spaces.
Examples of representing our community include using an official e-mail address,
posting via an official social media account, or acting as an appointed
representative at an online or offline event.

## Enforcement

Instances of abusive, harassing, or otherwise unacceptable behavior may be
reported to the community leaders responsible for enforcement at
[INSERT CONTACT METHOD].
All complaints will be reviewed and investigated promptly and fairly.

All community leaders are obligated to respect the privacy and security of the
reporter of any incident.

## Enforcement Guidelines

Community leaders will follow these Community Impact Guidelines in determining
the consequences for any action they deem in violation of this Code of Conduct:

### 1. Correction

**Community Impact**: Use of inappropriate language or other behavior deemed
unprofessional or unwelcome in the community.

**Consequence**: A private, written warning from community leaders, providing
clarity around the nature of the violation and an explanation of why the
behavior was inappropriate. A public apology may be requested.

### 2. Warning

**Community Impact**: A violation through a single incident or series of
actions.

**Consequence**: A warning with consequences for continued behavior. No
interaction with the people involved, including unsolicited interaction with
those enforcing the Code of Conduct, for a specified period of time. This
includes avoiding interactions in community spaces as well as external channels
like social media. Violating these terms may lead to a temporary or permanent
ban.

### 3. Temporary Ban

**Community Impact**: A serious violation of community standards, including
sustained inappropriate behavior.

**Consequence**: A temporary ban from any sort of interaction or public
communication with the community for a specified period of time. No public or
private interaction with the people involved, including unsolicited interaction
with those enforcing the Code of Conduct, is allowed during this period.
Violating these terms may lead to a permanent ban.

### 4. Permanent Ban

**Community Impact**: Demonstrating a pattern of violation of community
standards, including sustained inappropriate behavior, harassment of an
individual, or aggression toward or disparagement of classes of individuals.

**Consequence**: A permanent ban from any sort of public interaction within the
community.

## Attribution

This Code of Conduct is adapted from the [Contributor Covenant][homepage],
version 2.1, available at
[https://www.contributor-covenant.org/version/2/1/code_of_conduct.html][v2.1].

Community Impact Guidelines were inspired by
[Mozilla's code of conduct enforcement ladder][Mozilla CoC].

For answers to common questions about this code of conduct, see the FAQ at
[https://www.contributor-covenant.org/faq][FAQ]. Translations are available at
[https://www.contributor-covenant.org/translations][translations].

[homepage]: https://www.contributor-covenant.org

[Mozilla CoC]: https://github.com/mozilla/diversity

[translations]: https://www.contributor-covenant.org/translations

`CONTRIBUTING.md`:

```md
# Contributing

Contributions are always welcome, no matter how large or small!

We want this community to be friendly and respectful to each other. Please follow it in all your interactions with the project. Before contributing, please read the [code of conduct](./CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md).

## Development workflow

This project is a monorepo managed using [Yarn workspaces](https://yarnpkg.com/features/workspaces). It contains the following packages:

- The library package in the root directory.
- An example app in the `example/` directory.

To get started with the project, make sure you have the correct version of [Node.js](https://nodejs.org/) installed. See the [`.nvmrc`](./.nvmrc) file for the version used in this project.

Run `yarn` in the root directory to install the required dependencies for each package:

```sh
yarn
```

> Since the project relies on Yarn workspaces, you cannot use [`npm`](https://github.com/npm/cli) for development without manually migrating.

This project uses Nitro Modules. If you're not familiar with how Nitro works, make sure to check the [Nitro Modules Docs](https://nitro.margelo.com/).

You need to run [Nitrogen](https://nitro.margelo.com/docs/nitrogen) to generate the boilerplate code required for this project. The example app will not build without this step.

Run **Nitrogen** in following cases:

- When you make changes to any `*.nitro.ts` files.
- When running the project for the first time (since the generated files are not committed to the repository).

To invoke **Nitrogen**, use the following command:

```sh
yarn nitrogen
```

The [example app](/example/) demonstrates usage of the library. You need to run it to test any changes you make.

It is configured to use the local version of the library, so any changes you make to the library's source code will be reflected in the example app. Changes to the library's JavaScript code will be reflected in the example app without a rebuild, but native code changes will require a rebuild of the example app.

If you want to use Android Studio or Xcode to edit the native code, you can open the `example/android` or `example/ios` directories respectively in those editors. To edit the Objective-C or Swift files, open `example/ios/SecurityExample.xcworkspace` in Xcode and find the source files at `Pods > Development Pods > @rs-native-kit/security`.

To edit the Java or Kotlin files, open `example/android` in Android studio and find the source files at `rs-native-kit-security` under `Android`.

You can use various commands from the root directory to work with the project.

To start the packager:

```sh
yarn example start
```

To run the example app on Android:

```sh
yarn example android
```

To run the example app on iOS:

```sh
yarn example ios
```

To confirm that the app is running with the new architecture, you can check the Metro logs for a message like this:

```sh
Running "SecurityExample" with {"fabric":true,"initialProps":{"concurrentRoot":true},"rootTag":1}
```

Note the `"fabric":true` and `"concurrentRoot":true` properties.

To run the example app on Web:

```sh
yarn example web
```

Make sure your code passes TypeScript:

```sh
yarn typecheck
```

To check for linting errors, run the following:

```sh
yarn lint
```

To fix formatting errors, run the following:

```sh
yarn lint --fix
```

Remember to add tests for your change if possible. Run the unit tests by:

```sh
yarn test
```


### Commit message convention

We follow the [conventional commits specification](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/en) for our commit messages:

- `fix`: bug fixes, e.g. fix crash due to deprecated method.
- `feat`: new features, e.g. add new method to the module.
- `refactor`: code refactor, e.g. migrate from class components to hooks.
- `docs`: changes into documentation, e.g. add usage example for the module.
- `test`: adding or updating tests, e.g. add integration tests using detox.
- `chore`: tooling changes, e.g. change CI config.

Our pre-commit hooks verify that your commit message matches this format when committing.


### Publishing to npm

We use [release-it](https://github.com/release-it/release-it) to make it easier to publish new versions. It handles common tasks like bumping version based on semver, creating tags and releases etc.

To publish new versions locally, run the following:

```sh
yarn release
```

Releases can also be run from CI via the manually-triggered [`Release` workflow](.github/workflows/release.yml) (`workflow_dispatch`, defaults to a dry run). It requires a GitHub **environment** named `npm-publish` (Settings → Environments) configured with:

- An `NPM_TOKEN` secret (an npm automation token with publish access to `@rs-native-kit/security`).
- Optionally, required reviewers, so every publish gets a manual approval gate before it runs.

Nothing publishes automatically on push — every release is a deliberate, manually-triggered action.


### Scripts

The `package.json` file contains various scripts for common tasks:

- `yarn`: setup project by installing dependencies.
- `yarn typecheck`: type-check files with TypeScript.
  - `yarn lint`: lint files with [ESLint](https://eslint.org/).
    - `yarn test`: run unit tests with [Jest](https://jestjs.io/).
  - `yarn example start`: start the Metro server for the example app.
- `yarn example android`: run the example app on Android.
- `yarn example ios`: run the example app on iOS.
  - `yarn example web`: run the example app on Web.
- `yarn example build:web`: build the example app for Web.
  
### Sending a pull request

> **Working on your first pull request?** You can learn how from this _free_ series: [How to Contribute to an Open Source Project on GitHub](https://app.egghead.io/playlists/how-to-contribute-to-an-open-source-project-on-github).

When you're sending a pull request:

- Prefer small pull requests focused on one change.
- Verify that linters and tests are passing.
- Review the documentation to make sure it looks good.
- Follow the pull request template when opening a pull request.
- For pull requests that change the API or implementation, discuss with maintainers first by opening an issue.

```

`README.md`:

```md
# @rs-native-kit/security

[![npm version](https://img.shields.io/npm/v/@rs-native-kit/security.svg)](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@rs-native-kit/security)
[![npm downloads](https://img.shields.io/npm/dm/@rs-native-kit/security.svg)](https://www.npmjs.com/package/@rs-native-kit/security)
[![License: MIT](https://img.shields.io/badge/license-MIT-blue.svg)](LICENSE)
[![PRs Welcome](https://img.shields.io/badge/PRs-welcome-brightgreen.svg)](CONTRIBUTING.md)
[![Platform: iOS | Android](https://img.shields.io/badge/platform-iOS%20%7C%20Android-lightgrey.svg)](#installation)

**Root detection, jailbreak detection, and mobile app security for React Native.** Enterprise-grade security SDK for Android and iOS — root/jailbreak/emulator/hook detection, tamper & signature integrity, VPN/proxy/screen monitoring, SSL pinning helpers, and a configurable device risk engine. Built on [Nitro Modules](https://nitro.margelo.com/) for the New Architecture: JSI-direct calls, no bridge serialization, thread-safe by design.

If you're evaluating a **React Native root detection library**, a **jailbreak detection SDK**, or an **RASP (runtime application self-protection)** solution for a banking, fintech, or otherwise security-sensitive app, this covers root/jailbreak, Frida/Xposed/Magisk hook detection, tamper/integrity checks, SSL pinning, and a scored device-risk engine in one package.

> **Status:** functional on Android (root/emulator/hook/network/screen/integrity — verified via real device+emulator builds) and iOS (jailbreak/simulator/hook/network/screen/integrity — verified via simulator+device builds). Read [Security Notes](docs/SECURITY_NOTES.md) before shipping to production, especially for regulated (banking/healthcare) apps — no on-device SDK is a substitute for server-side verification.

## Features

- **Root detection (Android):** su binary scan, BusyBox, Magisk, RW `/system`, dangerous build props, SELinux state, suspicious mounts, known root-manager apps.
- **Jailbreak detection (iOS):** suspicious file scan, Cydia URL scheme, sandbox-escape write test, loaded-dylib inspection, process-spawn probe, suspicious env vars.
- **Emulator / Simulator detection** on both platforms.
- **Hook detection:** Frida (both platforms), Xposed/LSPosed/EdXposed/Substrate/Riru (Android).
- **Developer/debug state:** developer mode, USB debugging, attached debugger, mock location.
- **Network monitoring:** VPN and proxy detection.
- **Screen monitoring:** screen recording/mirroring, screenshot detection.
- **Overlay exposure** (Android).
- **Integrity:** app signature hash + verification, APK integrity (Android), tamper heuristics.
- **SSL pinning helpers:** typed certificate-pin builder/validator utilities.
- **Device risk engine:** configurable weighted scoring → `LOW` / `MEDIUM` / `HIGH` / `CRITICAL`.
- **Real-time event system:** subscribe to root/jailbreak/VPN/proxy/screenshot/hook/debugger/tamper events as they happen.

## Installation

```sh
npm install @rs-native-kit/security react-native-nitro-modules
```

`react-native-nitro-modules` is a required peer dependency — see [Installation](docs/INSTALLATION.md) for platform setup (Podfile, permissions, ProGuard/R8).

## Quick start

```ts
import Security from '@rs-native-kit/security';

const rooted = await Security.isRooted();
const jailbroken = await Security.isJailbroken();
const report = await Security.scan();

console.log(report.riskLevel, report.score, report.secure);
```

```ts
const unsubscribe = Security.addListener((event) => {
  console.log(event.type, event.message);
});
```

See the [API reference](docs/API.md) for the full surface and [Examples](docs/EXAMPLES.md) for common integration patterns (login gating, risk-based step-up auth, dashboards).

## Documentation

- [Installation](docs/INSTALLATION.md)
- [API Reference](docs/API.md)
- [Examples](docs/EXAMPLES.md)
- [Migration Guide](docs/MIGRATION.md)
- [Security Notes](docs/SECURITY_NOTES.md)
- [FAQ](docs/FAQ.md)
- [Troubleshooting](docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md)
- [Best Practices](docs/BEST_PRACTICES.md)

## Architecture

- **Nitro Modules, not hand-written TurboModules.** Nitro is a JSI-based, New-Architecture-only module system: synchronous-capable, strongly-typed, no bridge serialization. It satisfies the intent behind "TurboModules/Fabric support" better than a manual TurboModule codegen would for a module with no native UI component (Fabric doesn't apply here — there's nothing to render).
- **One native `Security` HybridObject**, composed internally from focused detector classes (`RootDetector`, `EmulatorDetector`, `HookDetector`, ... — see `android/src/main/java/.../detectors` and `ios/Detectors`), so the native code stays modular without multiplying JS↔native bridge objects.
- **The risk engine lives in TypeScript** (`src/risk/`), not native — native `scan()` returns raw `checks`/`reasons`, and the JS-side `RiskEngine` computes `score`/`riskLevel`/`secure` from configurable weights/thresholds. This lets you tune scoring without a native rebuild.
- **Every category is independently importable**: `import { isRooted } from '@rs-native-kit/security/root'`-style trees under `src/{root,jailbreak,emulator,...}`, so bundlers can tree-shake unused checks.

## Example app

`example/` is a working dashboard (security score, per-check grid, live event log, refresh button) — see [Examples](docs/EXAMPLES.md#running-the-example-app) to run it.

`SECURITY.md`:

```md
# Security Policy

This document covers reporting a vulnerability **in this SDK itself** (e.g. a detection bypass, a memory-safety issue in the native code, a bug that causes it to falsely report a device as secure). For guidance on the SDK's detection *capabilities and limitations* when integrating it into your own app, see [docs/SECURITY_NOTES.md](docs/SECURITY_NOTES.md).

## Supported versions

| Version | Supported |
|---|---|
| Latest `0.x` release | ✅ |
| Older `0.x` releases | Best-effort only |

Until `1.0.0`, only the latest published version receives security fixes — see [docs/MIGRATION.md](docs/MIGRATION.md#version-policy).

## Reporting a vulnerability

**Do not open a public GitHub issue for a security vulnerability.** Instead:

1. Use [GitHub's private vulnerability reporting](https://github.com/rajssinde/rs-native-kit-security/security/advisories/new) for this repository, if enabled, **or**
2. Email the maintainer listed in [package.json](package.json) with a description of the issue, steps to reproduce, and (if applicable) affected versions.

Please include:

- The affected platform(s) (Android/iOS) and OS/RN/library versions.
- Whether the issue is a **detection bypass** (a way to defeat a check that shouldn't be defeatable given this SDK's documented threat model — see [Security Notes](docs/SECURITY_NOTES.md#threat-model)), a **false "secure" report**, or a **native code issue** (crash, memory safety).
- A minimal reproduction if possible.

We aim to acknowledge reports within 5 business days.

## Scope

**In scope:**
- The SDK falsely reporting a device as secure/not-tampered/not-hooked when the opposite is true, for any signal within its documented [threat model](docs/SECURITY_NOTES.md#threat-model).
- Memory-safety, crash, or injection vulnerabilities in the native (Kotlin/Swift/C++) code.
- Vulnerabilities in the risk-scoring logic that could be trivially exploited to always land on `LOW`/`secure: true`.

**Out of scope (already documented, not vulnerabilities):**
- A statically-patched app binary defeating an in-process check — this is a fundamental limitation of any client-side detection SDK, not a bug in this one. See [Security Notes](docs/SECURITY_NOTES.md#threat-model).
- Platform checks that are hard-coded `false`/`true` because the underlying concept doesn't exist on that platform (e.g. `isRooted()` on iOS) — this is documented, intentional behavior, not a false negative.

```

# Set up local includes
include_directories("src/main/cpp" "../cpp")

find_library(LOG_LIB log)

# Link all libraries together

        ${PACKAGE_NAME}
        ${LOG_LIB}
        android # <-- Android core
)

`android/src/main/java/com/margelo/nitro/rsnativekit/security/Security.kt`:

```kt
package com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security

import com.facebook.proguard.annotations.DoNotStrip
import com.margelo.nitro.NitroModules
import com.margelo.nitro.core.Promise
import com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors.BusyBoxDetector
import com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors.DeveloperModeDetector
import com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors.EmulatorDetector
import com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors.HookDetector
import com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors.IntegrityChecker
import com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors.MagiskDetector
import com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors.NetworkMonitor
import com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors.OverlayDetector
import com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors.RootDetector
import com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors.ScreenMonitor
import com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.monitoring.SecurityMonitor

/**
 * Composition root: implements the Nitro `Security` spec by delegating to
 * the individual detectors under `detectors/`, and the event system to
 * `monitoring/SecurityMonitor`. Every detector does its own I/O
 * (file/process/PackageManager access), so every method here runs via
 * `Promise.parallel` — detection work never touches the JS/UI thread.
 */
@DoNotStrip
class Security : HybridSecuritySpec() {

  private val context by lazy {
    NitroModules.applicationContext
      ?: throw IllegalStateException(
        "[@rs-native-kit/security] No ReactApplicationContext is available yet."
      )
  }

  private val rootDetector by lazy { RootDetector(context) }
  private val magiskDetector by lazy { MagiskDetector(context) }
  private val busyBoxDetector by lazy { BusyBoxDetector() }
  private val emulatorDetector by lazy { EmulatorDetector(context) }
  private val hookDetector by lazy { HookDetector(context) }
  private val developerModeDetector by lazy { DeveloperModeDetector(context) }
  private val networkMonitor by lazy { NetworkMonitor(context) }
  private val overlayDetector by lazy { OverlayDetector(context) }
  private val screenMonitor by lazy { ScreenMonitor(context) }
  private val integrityChecker by lazy { IntegrityChecker(context) }

  @Volatile
  private var eventListener: ((NativeSecurityEvent) -> Unit)? = null

  private val monitor by lazy {
    SecurityMonitor(
      context = context,
      rootDetector = rootDetector,
      hookDetector = hookDetector,
      integrityChecker = integrityChecker,
      networkMonitor = networkMonitor,
      screenMonitor = screenMonitor,
      developerModeDetector = developerModeDetector
    ) { event ->
      eventListener?.invoke(NativeSecurityEvent(event.type, event.message, event.timestamp))
    }
  }

  override fun isRooted(): Promise<Boolean> = Promise.parallel { rootDetector.isRooted() }

  override fun isMagiskDetected(): Promise<Boolean> =
    Promise.parallel { magiskDetector.isDetected() }

  override fun isBusyBoxDetected(): Promise<Boolean> =
    Promise.parallel { busyBoxDetector.isDetected() }

  /** Jailbreak is an iOS-only concept; always `false` on Android. */
  override fun isJailbroken(): Promise<Boolean> = Promise.resolved(false)

  override fun isEmulator(): Promise<Boolean> = Promise.parallel { emulatorDetector.isDetected() }

  override fun isDeveloperModeEnabled(): Promise<Boolean> =
    Promise.parallel { developerModeDetector.isDeveloperModeEnabled() }

  override fun isUsbDebuggingEnabled(): Promise<Boolean> =
    Promise.parallel { developerModeDetector.isUsbDebuggingEnabled() }

  override fun isDebuggerAttached(): Promise<Boolean> =
    Promise.parallel { developerModeDetector.isDebuggerAttached() }

  override fun isMockLocationEnabled(): Promise<Boolean> =
    Promise.parallel { developerModeDetector.isMockLocationEnabled() }

  override fun isVpnConnected(): Promise<Boolean> =
    Promise.parallel { networkMonitor.isVpnConnected() }

  override fun isProxyEnabled(): Promise<Boolean> =
    Promise.parallel { networkMonitor.isProxyEnabled() }

  override fun isScreenRecording(): Promise<Boolean> =
    Promise.parallel { screenMonitor.isScreenRecording() }

  override fun isScreenCaptured(): Promise<Boolean> =
    Promise.parallel { screenMonitor.isScreenCaptured() }

  override fun hasOverlayPermission(): Promise<Boolean> =
    Promise.parallel { overlayDetector.hasOverlayPermission() }

  override fun isHookDetected(): Promise<Boolean> =
    Promise.parallel { hookDetector.isAnyHookDetected() }

  override fun isFridaDetected(): Promise<Boolean> =
    Promise.parallel { hookDetector.isFridaDetected() }

  override fun isXposedDetected(): Promise<Boolean> =
    Promise.parallel { hookDetector.isXposedDetected() }

  override fun isLSPosedDetected(): Promise<Boolean> =
    Promise.parallel { hookDetector.isLSPosedDetected() }

  override fun isTampered(): Promise<Boolean> = Promise.parallel { integrityChecker.isTampered() }

  override fun getAppSignatureHash(): Promise<String> =
    Promise.parallel { integrityChecker.getAppSignatureHash() }

  override fun verifyAppSignature(expectedSignatureHash: String): Promise<Boolean> =
    Promise.parallel { integrityChecker.verifyAppSignature(expectedSignatureHash) }

  override fun verifyApkIntegrity(expectedSha256: String): Promise<Boolean> =
    Promise.parallel { integrityChecker.verifyApkIntegrity(expectedSha256) }

  /** Android's TLS stack (OkHttp/`URLConnection`) always supports pinning; this is informational. */
  override fun isSslPinningSupported(): Promise<Boolean> = Promise.resolved(true)

  override fun scan(): Promise<NativeScanResult> = Promise.parallel {
    val reasons = mutableListOf<String>()

    val rootReasons = rootDetector.reasons()
    reasons += rootReasons
    val isRootedValue = rootReasons.isNotEmpty()

    val isMagisk = magiskDetector.isDetected()
    if (isMagisk) reasons += "Magisk detected"

    val isBusyBox = busyBoxDetector.isDetected()
    if (isBusyBox) reasons += "BusyBox binary detected"

    val isEmu = emulatorDetector.isDetected()
    if (isEmu) reasons += "Running on an emulator"

    val isFrida = hookDetector.isFridaDetected()
    if (isFrida) reasons += "Frida detected"

    val isXposed = hookDetector.isXposedDetected()
    if (isXposed) reasons += "Xposed/EdXposed detected"

    val isLSPosed = hookDetector.isLSPosedDetected()
    if (isLSPosed) reasons += "LSPosed detected"

    val isSubstrateOrRiru = hookDetector.isSubstrateOrRiruDetected()
    if (isSubstrateOrRiru) reasons += "Substrate/Riru injection layer detected"

    val hookAny = isFrida || isXposed || isLSPosed || isSubstrateOrRiru

    val devMode = developerModeDetector.isDeveloperModeEnabled()
    if (devMode) reasons += "Developer mode enabled"

    val usbDebug = developerModeDetector.isUsbDebuggingEnabled()
    if (usbDebug) reasons += "USB debugging enabled"

    val debuggerAttached = developerModeDetector.isDebuggerAttached()
    if (debuggerAttached) reasons += "Debugger attached to process"

    val mockLoc = developerModeDetector.isMockLocationEnabled()
    if (mockLoc) reasons += "Mock location provider enabled"

    val vpn = networkMonitor.isVpnConnected()
    if (vpn) reasons += "VPN connection active"

    val proxy = networkMonitor.isProxyEnabled()
    if (proxy) reasons += "System proxy configured"

    val overlay = overlayDetector.hasOverlayPermission()
    if (overlay) reasons += "Overlay draw permission/app detected"

    val screenRec = screenMonitor.isScreenRecording()
    if (screenRec) reasons += "Screen recording/mirroring signal detected"

    val screenCap = screenMonitor.isScreenCaptured()

    val tampered = integrityChecker.isTampered()
    if (tampered) reasons += "App tamper signal detected"

    val checks = NativeSecurityChecks(
      root = isRootedValue,
      jailbreak = false,
      emulator = isEmu,
      developerMode = devMode,
      usbDebugging = usbDebug,
      vpn = vpn,
      proxy = proxy,
      overlay = overlay,
      frida = isFrida,
      xposed = isXposed,
      lsposed = isLSPosed,
      magisk = isMagisk,
      busybox = isBusyBox,
      mockLocation = mockLoc,
      hookDetected = hookAny,
      tampered = tampered,
      // No app-supplied "known good" hash is available in a zero-config scan();
      // call verifyAppSignature() directly for an authoritative check.
      signatureValid = true,
      sslPinningSupported = true,
      screenRecording = screenRec,
      screenCaptured = screenCap,
      debuggerAttached = debuggerAttached
    )

    NativeScanResult(checks = checks, reasons = reasons.toTypedArray())
  }

  override fun setEventListener(listener: (event: NativeSecurityEvent) -> Unit) {
    eventListener = listener
  }

  override fun removeEventListener() {
    eventListener = null
  }

  override fun startMonitoring() {
    monitor.start()
  }

  override fun stopMonitoring() {
    monitor.stop()
  }
}
```

`android/src/main/java/com/margelo/nitro/rsnativekit/security/detectors/BusyBoxDetector.kt`:

```kt
package com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors

import com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.util.ShellUtils

/** BusyBox is a legitimate embedded-Linux tool, but its presence on a stock Android device is a root/custom-ROM signal. */
internal class BusyBoxDetector {
  fun isDetected(): Boolean {
    return ShellUtils.binaryExistsOnCommonPaths("busybox") ||
      ShellUtils.binaryExistsOnPath("busybox") ||
      ShellUtils.exec(arrayOf("busybox")) != null
  }
}
```

`android/src/main/java/com/margelo/nitro/rsnativekit/security/detectors/DeveloperModeDetector.kt`:

```kt
package com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors

import android.app.AppOpsManager
import android.content.Context
import android.content.pm.PackageManager
import android.os.Build
import android.os.Debug
import android.os.Process
import android.provider.Settings

internal class DeveloperModeDetector(private val context: Context) {

  fun isDeveloperModeEnabled(): Boolean {
    return try {
      Settings.Global.getInt(
        context.contentResolver,
        Settings.Global.DEVELOPMENT_SETTINGS_ENABLED,
        0
      ) != 0
    } catch (_: Exception) {
      false
    }
  }

  fun isUsbDebuggingEnabled(): Boolean {
    return try {
      Settings.Global.getInt(context.contentResolver, Settings.Global.ADB_ENABLED, 0) != 0
    } catch (_: Exception) {
      false
    }
  }

  /** Native debugger (JDWP/ptrace) attach detection — independent of any RN/JS debugger. */
  fun isDebuggerAttached(): Boolean {
    return Debug.isDebuggerConnected() || Debug.waitingForDebugger()
  }

  private val knownMockLocationApps = arrayOf(
    "com.lexa.fakegps",
    "com.incorporateapps.fakegps.fre",
    "com.blogspot.newapphorizons.fakegps",
    "io.github.jsonstat.fakelocation"
  )

  fun isMockLocationEnabled(): Boolean {
    return isMockLocationAppOpEnabled() || isLegacyMockLocationSettingEnabled() || hasMockLocationApp()
  }

  /** API 23+: `AppOpsManager` tracks whether this app was granted the mock-location app-op. */
  @Suppress("DEPRECATION")
  private fun isMockLocationAppOpEnabled(): Boolean {
    if (Build.VERSION.SDK_INT < Build.VERSION_CODES.M) return false
    return try {
      val appOps = context.getSystemService(Context.APP_OPS_SERVICE) as? AppOpsManager
        ?: return false
      val mode = appOps.checkOpNoThrow(
        AppOpsManager.OPSTR_MOCK_LOCATION,
        Process.myUid(),
        context.packageName
      )
      mode == AppOpsManager.MODE_ALLOWED
    } catch (_: Exception) {
      false
    }
  }

  /** Pre-API-23 fallback: the (now-removed) `Settings.Secure.ALLOW_MOCK_LOCATION` flag. */
  @Suppress("DEPRECATION")
  private fun isLegacyMockLocationSettingEnabled(): Boolean {
    if (Build.VERSION.SDK_INT >= Build.VERSION_CODES.M) return false
    return try {
      Settings.Secure.getString(context.contentResolver, Settings.Secure.ALLOW_MOCK_LOCATION) != "0"
    } catch (_: Exception) {
      false
    }
  }

  private fun hasMockLocationApp(): Boolean {
    val pm = context.packageManager
    return knownMockLocationApps.any { pkg ->
      try {
        pm.getPackageInfo(pkg, 0)
        true
      } catch (_: PackageManager.NameNotFoundException) {
        false
      }
    }
  }
}
```

`android/src/main/java/com/margelo/nitro/rsnativekit/security/detectors/EmulatorDetector.kt`:

```kt
package com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors

import android.content.Context
import android.content.pm.PackageManager
import android.os.Build
import java.io.File

/**
 * Detects the generic Android Emulator/AVD plus the common third-party
 * emulators (Genymotion, BlueStacks, LDPlayer, Nox, MEmu) via `Build.*`
 * fingerprints, well-known emulator-only files, and installed packages.
 */
internal class EmulatorDetector(private val context: Context) {

  private val qemuFiles = arrayOf(
    "/dev/qemu_pipe",
    "/dev/socket/qemud",
    "/system/lib/libc_malloc_debug_qemu.so",
    "/sys/qemu_trace",
    "/system/bin/qemu-props"
  )

  private val emulatorPackages = arrayOf(
    "com.genymotion.genyd", // Genymotion
    "com.bluestacks", // BlueStacks
    "com.bluestacks.home",
    "com.vphone.launcher", // Nox
    "com.bignox.app",
    "com.microvirt.launcher", // MEmu
    "com.microvirt.market",
    "com.ldmnq.launcher3" // LDPlayer
  )

  fun isDetected(): Boolean {
    return hasEmulatorBuildFingerprint() ||
      hasEmulatorFiles() ||
      hasEmulatorPackages() ||
      hasEmulatorTelephonyProperties()
  }

  private fun hasEmulatorBuildFingerprint(): Boolean {
    val fingerprint = Build.FINGERPRINT.lowercase()
    val model = Build.MODEL.lowercase()
    val manufacturer = Build.MANUFACTURER.lowercase()
    val hardware = Build.HARDWARE.lowercase()
    val product = Build.PRODUCT.lowercase()
    val brand = Build.BRAND.lowercase()

    return fingerprint.startsWith("generic") ||
      fingerprint.startsWith("unknown") ||
      fingerprint.contains("vbox") ||
      fingerprint.contains("test-keys") && model.contains("sdk") ||
      model.contains("google_sdk") ||
      model.contains("emulator") ||
      model.contains("android sdk built for x86") ||
      manufacturer.contains("genymotion") ||
      manufacturer == "unknown" && brand.startsWith("generic") ||
      hardware.contains("goldfish") ||
      hardware.contains("ranchu") ||
      hardware.contains("vbox86") ||
      product.contains("sdk_gphone") ||
      product.contains("google_sdk") ||
      product.contains("sdk_x86") ||
      product.contains("vbox86p") ||
      Build.HOST.startsWith("Build")
  }

  private fun hasEmulatorFiles(): Boolean {
    return qemuFiles.any { File(it).exists() }
  }

  private fun hasEmulatorPackages(): Boolean {
    val pm = context.packageManager
    return emulatorPackages.any { pkg ->
      try {
        pm.getPackageInfo(pkg, 0)
        true
      } catch (_: PackageManager.NameNotFoundException) {
        false
      }
    }
  }

  private fun hasEmulatorTelephonyProperties(): Boolean {
    // Stock AVDs consistently report these placeholder telephony identifiers.
    return try {
      val tm = context.getSystemService(Context.TELEPHONY_SERVICE) as? android.telephony.TelephonyManager
      val networkOperatorName = tm?.networkOperatorName?.lowercase()
      networkOperatorName == "android"
    } catch (_: SecurityException) {
      false
    } catch (_: Exception) {
      false
    }
  }
}
```

`android/src/main/java/com/margelo/nitro/rsnativekit/security/detectors/HookDetector.kt`:

```kt
package com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors

import android.content.Context
import android.content.pm.PackageManager
import com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.util.ShellUtils
import java.io.File
import java.net.InetSocketAddress
import java.net.Socket

/**
 * Detects the most common Android instrumentation/hooking frameworks:
 * Frida, Xposed, LSPosed, EdXposed, Cydia Substrate and Riru (the Zygote
 * injection layer several of the above build on).
 */
internal class HookDetector(private val context: Context) {

  // --- Frida -----------------------------------------------------------------

  private val fridaDefaultPorts = intArrayOf(27042, 27043)

  fun isFridaDetected(): Boolean {
    return hasFridaPortOpen() || hasFridaArtifactsInMaps() || hasFridaThreads()
  }

  private fun hasFridaPortOpen(): Boolean {
    return fridaDefaultPorts.any { port ->
      try {
        Socket().use { socket ->
          socket.connect(InetSocketAddress("127.0.0.1", port), 200)
          true
        }
      } catch (_: Exception) {
        false
      }
    }
  }

  private fun hasFridaArtifactsInMaps(): Boolean {
    val maps = ShellUtils.readFileOrNull("/proc/self/maps") ?: return false
    val markers = listOf("frida-agent", "frida-gadget", "gum-js-loop", "linjector")
    return markers.any { maps.contains(it) }
  }

  private fun hasFridaThreads(): Boolean {
    val tasksDir = File("/proc/self/task")
    val threadDirs = tasksDir.listFiles() ?: return false
    val markers = listOf("gum-js-loop", "gmain", "gdbus", "frida")
    return threadDirs.any { dir ->
      val comm = ShellUtils.readFileOrNull(File(dir, "comm").path)?.trim()
      comm != null && markers.any { comm.contains(it) }
    }
  }

  // --- Xposed / EdXposed --------------------------------------------------------

  private val xposedPackages = arrayOf(
    "de.robv.android.xposed.installer",
    "org.meowcat.edxposed.manager",
    "com.solohsu.android.edxp.manager"
  )

  fun isXposedDetected(): Boolean {
    return isClassPresent("de.robv.android.xposed.XposedBridge") ||
      isClassPresent("de.robv.android.xposed.XC_MethodHook") ||
      File("/system/framework/XposedBridge.jar").exists() ||
      isAnyPackageInstalled(xposedPackages)
  }

  // --- LSPosed -----------------------------------------------------------------

  private val lsposedPackages = arrayOf(
    "org.lsposed.manager"
  )

  private val lsposedPaths = arrayOf(
    "/data/adb/lspd",
    "/data/misc/lspd",
    "/data/adb/modules/zygisk_lsposed"
  )

  fun isLSPosedDetected(): Boolean {
    return isClassPresent("org.lsposed.lspd.core.Main") ||
      isAnyPackageInstalled(lsposedPackages) ||
      lsposedPaths.any { File(it).exists() }
  }

  // --- Substrate / Riru ------------------------------------------------------------

  private val riruPaths = arrayOf(
    "/data/misc/riru",
    "/data/adb/riru",
    "/data/adb/modules/riru-core"
  )

  fun isSubstrateOrRiruDetected(): Boolean {
    return isAnyPackageInstalled(arrayOf("com.saurik.substrate")) ||
      File("/data/data/com.saurik.substrate").exists() ||
      riruPaths.any { File(it).exists() } ||
      hasSubstrateArtifactsInMaps()
  }

  private fun hasSubstrateArtifactsInMaps(): Boolean {
    val maps = ShellUtils.readFileOrNull("/proc/self/maps") ?: return false
    return maps.contains("libsubstrate") || maps.contains("libsubstrate-dvm")
  }

  /** Umbrella check combining every hooking-framework signal. */
  fun isAnyHookDetected(): Boolean {
    return isFridaDetected() ||
      isXposedDetected() ||
      isLSPosedDetected() ||
      isSubstrateOrRiruDetected()
  }

  private fun isClassPresent(className: String): Boolean {
    return try {
      Class.forName(className)
      true
    } catch (_: ClassNotFoundException) {
      false
    } catch (_: Exception) {
      false
    }
  }

  private fun isAnyPackageInstalled(packages: Array<String>): Boolean {
    val pm = context.packageManager
    return packages.any { pkg ->
      try {
        pm.getPackageInfo(pkg, 0)
        true
      } catch (_: PackageManager.NameNotFoundException) {
        false
      }
    }
  }
}
```

`android/src/main/java/com/margelo/nitro/rsnativekit/security/detectors/IntegrityChecker.kt`:

```kt
package com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors

import android.content.Context
import android.content.pm.ApplicationInfo
import android.content.pm.PackageManager
import android.os.Build
import java.io.File
import java.security.MessageDigest

/**
 * Signature/APK integrity + a zero-config tamper heuristic.
 *
 * `isTampered()` needs to work without any app-supplied "known good" hash
 * (it's part of the parameterless `scan()`), so it can only check for
 * generic tamper/repackaging *symptoms* (debuggable release build, missing
 * installer identity). For an authoritative check, apps should call
 * [verifyAppSignature] or [verifyApkIntegrity] with a hash pinned at build
 * time.
 */
internal class IntegrityChecker(private val context: Context) {

  fun isTampered(): Boolean {
    return isDebuggableReleaseBuild() || hasUntrustedInstaller()
  }

  private fun isDebuggableReleaseBuild(): Boolean {
    return (context.applicationInfo.flags and ApplicationInfo.FLAG_DEBUGGABLE) != 0
  }

  private val trustedInstallers = setOf(
    "com.android.vending", // Google Play
    "com.amazon.venezia", // Amazon Appstore
    "com.sec.android.app.samsungapps", // Galaxy Store
    "com.huawei.appmarket"
  )

  /**
   * Flags apps with *no* installer identity at all (typical of a raw
   * `adb install`/direct APK sideload of a repackaged app). Enterprise
   * MDM-distributed installs use a real installer package too (the MDM
   * agent), so this doesn't penalize legitimate internal distribution.
   */
  private fun hasUntrustedInstaller(): Boolean {
    val installer = getInstallerPackageName() ?: return true
    return installer.isBlank()
  }

  private fun getInstallerPackageName(): String? {
    return try {
      val pm = context.packageManager
      if (Build.VERSION.SDK_INT >= Build.VERSION_CODES.R) {
        pm.getInstallSourceInfo(context.packageName).installingPackageName
      } else {
        @Suppress("DEPRECATION")
        pm.getInstallerPackageName(context.packageName)
      }
    } catch (_: Exception) {
      null
    }
  }

  /** Not currently used to compute [isTampered], but kept for future stricter policies. */
  internal fun isInstalledFromTrustedStore(): Boolean {
    return getInstallerPackageName() in trustedInstallers
  }

  fun getAppSignatureHash(): String {
    val digestBytes = getSigningCertificateBytes()
      ?: throw IllegalStateException("Unable to read the app's signing certificate")
    return sha256Hex(digestBytes)
  }

  fun verifyAppSignature(expectedSignatureHash: String): Boolean {
    return try {
      getAppSignatureHash().equals(expectedSignatureHash, ignoreCase = true)
    } catch (_: Exception) {
      false
    }
  }

  fun verifyApkIntegrity(expectedSha256: String): Boolean {
    val apkPath = context.applicationInfo.sourceDir
    val digest = sha256HexOfFile(apkPath) ?: return false
    return digest.equals(expectedSha256, ignoreCase = true)
  }

  @Suppress("DEPRECATION")
  private fun getSigningCertificateBytes(): ByteArray? {
    return try {
      val pm = context.packageManager
      if (Build.VERSION.SDK_INT >= Build.VERSION_CODES.P) {
        val info = pm.getPackageInfo(context.packageName, PackageManager.GET_SIGNING_CERTIFICATES)
        val signingInfo = info.signingInfo ?: return null
        val signatures = if (signingInfo.hasMultipleSigners()) {
          signingInfo.apkContentsSigners
        } else {
          signingInfo.signingCertificateHistory
        }
        signatures?.firstOrNull()?.toByteArray()
      } else {
        val info = pm.getPackageInfo(context.packageName, PackageManager.GET_SIGNATURES)
        info.signatures?.firstOrNull()?.toByteArray()
      }
    } catch (_: Exception) {
      null
    }
  }

  private fun sha256Hex(bytes: ByteArray): String {
    val digest = MessageDigest.getInstance("SHA-256").digest(bytes)
    return digest.joinToString("") { "%02x".format(it) }
  }

  private fun sha256HexOfFile(path: String): String? {
    return try {
      val digest = MessageDigest.getInstance("SHA-256")
      File(path).inputStream().use { input ->
        val buffer = ByteArray(8192)
        var read: Int
        while (input.read(buffer).also { read = it } != -1) {
          digest.update(buffer, 0, read)
        }
      }
      digest.digest().joinToString("") { "%02x".format(it) }
    } catch (_: Exception) {
      null
    }
  }
}
```

`android/src/main/java/com/margelo/nitro/rsnativekit/security/detectors/MagiskDetector.kt`:

```kt
package com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors

import android.content.Context
import android.content.pm.PackageManager
import com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.util.ShellUtils

/** Magisk-specific signals, separate from generic root heuristics so `checks.magisk` stays precise. */
internal class MagiskDetector(private val context: Context) {

  private val magiskPaths = arrayOf(
    "/sbin/.magisk",
    "/cache/.disable_magisk",
    "/dev/.magisk.unblock",
    "/cache/magisk.log",
    "/data/adb/magisk",
    "/data/adb/magisk.db",
    "/data/adb/modules",
    "/data/magisk.apk",
    "/data/adb/magisk_simple"
  )

  fun isDetected(): Boolean {
    return isMagiskAppInstalled() ||
      magiskPaths.any { path -> ShellUtils.readFileOrNull(path) != null || java.io.File(path).exists() } ||
      ShellUtils.binaryExistsOnCommonPaths("magisk") ||
      ShellUtils.exec(arrayOf("magisk", "-v")) != null
  }

  private fun isMagiskAppInstalled(): Boolean {
    return try {
      context.packageManager.getPackageInfo("com.topjohnwu.magisk", 0)
      true
    } catch (_: PackageManager.NameNotFoundException) {
      false
    }
  }
}

`android/src/main/java/com/margelo/nitro/rsnativekit/security/detectors/NetworkMonitor.kt`:

```kt
package com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors

import android.content.Context
import android.net.ConnectivityManager
import android.net.NetworkCapabilities
import android.os.Build
import java.net.NetworkInterface
import java.util.Collections

internal class NetworkMonitor(private val context: Context) {

  fun isVpnConnected(): Boolean {
    return isVpnActiveViaConnectivityManager() || hasVpnNetworkInterface()
  }

  private fun isVpnActiveViaConnectivityManager(): Boolean {
    if (Build.VERSION.SDK_INT < Build.VERSION_CODES.M) return false
    return try {
      val cm = context.getSystemService(Context.CONNECTIVITY_SERVICE) as? ConnectivityManager
        ?: return false
      val network = cm.activeNetwork ?: return false
      val capabilities = cm.getNetworkCapabilities(network) ?: return false
      capabilities.hasTransport(NetworkCapabilities.TRANSPORT_VPN)
    } catch (_: Exception) {
      false
    }
  }

  /** Fallback for pre-M devices (and a cross-check on newer ones): scan for tunnel interfaces. */
  private fun hasVpnNetworkInterface(): Boolean {
    return try {
      Collections.list(NetworkInterface.getNetworkInterfaces()).any { iface ->
        iface.isUp && (iface.name.startsWith("tun") || iface.name.startsWith("ppp") || iface.name.startsWith("pptp"))
      }
    } catch (_: Exception) {
      false
    }
  }

  fun isProxyEnabled(): Boolean {
    return hasSystemProxyProperty() || hasConnectivityManagerProxy()
  }

  private fun hasSystemProxyProperty(): Boolean {
    val host = System.getProperty("http.proxyHost")
    val port = System.getProperty("http.proxyPort")
    return !host.isNullOrEmpty() && !port.isNullOrEmpty()
  }

  private fun hasConnectivityManagerProxy(): Boolean {
    return try {
      val cm = context.getSystemService(Context.CONNECTIVITY_SERVICE) as? ConnectivityManager
        ?: return false
      val proxyInfo = cm.defaultProxy
      proxyInfo != null && !proxyInfo.host.isNullOrEmpty()
    } catch (_: Exception) {
      false
    }
  }
}
```

`android/src/main/java/com/margelo/nitro/rsnativekit/security/detectors/OverlayDetector.kt`:

```kt
package com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors

import android.content.Context
import android.content.pm.PackageManager
import android.os.Build
import android.provider.Settings

/**
 * Reports overlay/tapjacking exposure. Bulk-enumerating *other* installed
 * apps' `SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW` grants would require the heavily Play-Store-
 * gated `QUERY_ALL_PACKAGES` permission, so this stays to what's safe for a
 * library to ship: this app's own overlay-draw capability, plus targeted
 * (single-package, always-allowed) lookups for a short list of apps known
 * to abuse overlays for tapjacking / screen-lock bypass.
 */
internal class OverlayDetector(private val context: Context) {

  private val knownOverlayAbusePackages = arrayOf(
    "com.baiduAppSearch.searchbox", // historical overlay-abuse offenders used in public research
    "com.gtp.nextlauncher.overlay",
    "com.android.systemui.overlay.fake"
  )

  fun hasOverlayPermission(): Boolean {
    return canThisAppDrawOverlays() || hasKnownOverlayAbuseAppInstalled()
  }

  private fun canThisAppDrawOverlays(): Boolean {
    if (Build.VERSION.SDK_INT < Build.VERSION_CODES.M) return false
    return try {
      Settings.canDrawOverlays(context)
    } catch (_: Exception) {
      false
    }
  }

  private fun hasKnownOverlayAbuseAppInstalled(): Boolean {
    val pm = context.packageManager
    return knownOverlayAbusePackages.any { pkg ->
      try {
        pm.getPackageInfo(pkg, 0)
        true
      } catch (_: PackageManager.NameNotFoundException) {
        false
      }
    }
  }
}

`android/src/main/java/com/margelo/nitro/rsnativekit/security/detectors/RootDetector.kt`:

```kt
package com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors

import android.content.Context
import android.content.pm.PackageManager
import android.os.Build
import com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.util.ShellUtils

/**
 * Android root detection using multiple independent, individually-weak
 * signals — no single check is authoritative, so [isRooted] treats "two or
 * more distinct signals fired" as strong evidence, while [reasons] still
 * surfaces every individual signal for audit logging.
 */
internal class RootDetector(private val context: Context) {

  /** Known root-management / su-provider app packages. */
  private val rootPackages = arrayOf(
    "com.topjohnwu.magisk",
    "com.noshufou.android.su",
    "com.noshufou.android.su.elite",
    "eu.chainfire.supersu",
    "com.koushikdutta.superuser",
    "com.thirdparty.superuser",
    "com.yellowes.su",
    "com.kingroot.kinguser",
    "com.kingo.root",
    "com.smedialink.oneclickroot",
    "com.zhiqupk.root.global",
    "com.alephzain.framaroot",
    "me.phh.superuser",
    "com.geohot.towelroot",
    "com.ramdroid.appquarantine"
  )

  private val dangerousProps = mapOf(
    "ro.debuggable" to "1",
    "ro.secure" to "0"
  )

  fun isRooted(): Boolean = reasons().isNotEmpty()

  /** Human-readable reason for every root signal that fired, for `scan()`'s `reasons[]`. */
  fun reasons(): List<String> {
    val found = mutableListOf<String>()

    if (ShellUtils.binaryExistsOnCommonPaths("su") || ShellUtils.binaryExistsOnPath("su")) {
      found += "su binary found on device"
    }
    if (canExecuteSu()) {
      found += "su binary is executable"
    }
    if (Build.TAGS?.contains("test-keys") == true) {
      found += "Build.TAGS contains test-keys (custom/dev-signed ROM)"
    }
    if (hasDangerousBuildProps()) {
      found += "ro.debuggable=1 or ro.secure=0 system property detected"
    }
    if (hasRootManagementApp()) {
      found += "a known root-management app is installed"
    }
    if (isSystemWritable()) {
      found += "/system partition is writable (should be read-only)"
    }
    if (isSELinuxPermissive()) {
      found += "SELinux is permissive (should be enforcing)"
    }
    if (hasSuspiciousMounts()) {
      found += "root-hiding bind mount detected in /proc/self/mountinfo"
    }

    return found
  }

  private fun canExecuteSu(): Boolean {
    return ShellUtils.exec(arrayOf("which", "su")) != null ||
      ShellUtils.exec(arrayOf("su", "-c", "id")) != null
  }

  private fun hasDangerousBuildProps(): Boolean {
    val output = ShellUtils.exec(arrayOf("getprop")) ?: return false
    return dangerousProps.any { (key, value) ->
      output.contains("[$key]: [$value]")
    }
  }

  private fun hasRootManagementApp(): Boolean {
    val pm = context.packageManager
    return rootPackages.any { pkg -> isPackageInstalled(pm, pkg) }
  }

  private fun isPackageInstalled(pm: PackageManager, packageName: String): Boolean {
    return try {
      pm.getPackageInfo(packageName, 0)
      true
    } catch (_: PackageManager.NameNotFoundException) {
      false
    }
  }

  private fun isSystemWritable(): Boolean {
    return ShellUtils.isWritable("/system") || ShellUtils.isWritable("/system/bin")
  }

  private fun isSELinuxPermissive(): Boolean {
    val status = ShellUtils.readFileOrNull("/sys/fs/selinux/enforce")
      ?: ShellUtils.exec(arrayOf("getenforce"))
    return status?.trim()?.let { it == "0" || it.equals("Permissive", ignoreCase = true) } == true
  }

  private fun hasSuspiciousMounts(): Boolean {
    val mountInfo = ShellUtils.readFileOrNull("/proc/self/mountinfo") ?: return false
    return mountInfo.lineSequence().any { line ->
      (line.contains("/system") || line.contains("/vendor")) && line.contains(" rw,")
    }
  }
}
```

`android/src/main/java/com/margelo/nitro/rsnativekit/security/detectors/ScreenMonitor.kt`:

```kt
package com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors

import android.content.Context
import android.hardware.display.DisplayManager
import android.view.Display
import java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicLong

/**
 * Android has no direct, permission-less "is a third-party app recording my
 * screen right now" API (unlike iOS's `UIScreen.isCaptured`). [isScreenRecording]
 * is therefore a best-effort heuristic based on presentation/mirrored
 * displays; consumers that need a hard guarantee should combine this SDK
 * with `WindowManager.LayoutParams.FLAG_SECURE` on sensitive screens.
 *
 * [isScreenCaptured] reports whether a screenshot was observed recently —
 * it's driven by [markScreenshotDetected], called from the
 * `MediaStore`-observing half of the monitoring system.
 */
internal class ScreenMonitor(private val context: Context) {

  companion object {
    private const val RECENT_SCREENSHOT_WINDOW_MS = 5_000L
  }

  private val lastScreenshotAtMs = AtomicLong(0)

  fun isScreenRecording(): Boolean {
    return try {
      val displayManager =
        context.getSystemService(Context.DISPLAY_SERVICE) as? DisplayManager ?: return false
      displayManager.displays.any { display ->
        display.displayId != Display.DEFAULT_DISPLAY &&
          (display.flags and Display.FLAG_PRESENTATION) != 0
      }
    } catch (_: Exception) {
      false
    }
  }

  fun isScreenCaptured(): Boolean {
    val last = lastScreenshotAtMs.get()
    if (last == 0L) return false
    return (System.currentTimeMillis() - last) < RECENT_SCREENSHOT_WINDOW_MS
  }

  fun markScreenshotDetected() {
    lastScreenshotAtMs.set(System.currentTimeMillis())
  }
}
```

`android/src/main/java/com/margelo/nitro/rsnativekit/security/monitoring/SecurityMonitor.kt`:

```kt
package com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.monitoring

import android.content.Context
import android.database.ContentObserver
import android.net.ConnectivityManager
import android.net.Network
import android.net.NetworkCapabilities
import android.net.NetworkRequest
import android.os.Handler
import android.os.Looper
import android.provider.MediaStore
import com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors.DeveloperModeDetector
import com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors.HookDetector
import com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors.IntegrityChecker
import com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors.NetworkMonitor
import com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors.RootDetector
import com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.detectors.ScreenMonitor
import java.util.concurrent.Executors
import java.util.concurrent.ScheduledExecutorService
import java.util.concurrent.TimeUnit
import java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicBoolean

internal data class SecurityMonitorEvent(val type: String, val message: String, val timestamp: Double)

/**
 * Drives the JS event system. VPN and screenshot signals use real OS push
 * sources (`ConnectivityManager.NetworkCallback`, a `MediaStore`
 * `ContentObserver`); everything else that doesn't have a first-class
 * Android push API (root/hook/debugger/tamper/signature state) is
 * re-evaluated on a short poll, only emitting on a false→true transition so
 * a stable device doesn't spam listeners.
 */
internal class SecurityMonitor(
  private val context: Context,
  private val rootDetector: RootDetector,
  private val hookDetector: HookDetector,
  private val integrityChecker: IntegrityChecker,
  private val networkMonitor: NetworkMonitor,
  private val screenMonitor: ScreenMonitor,
  private val developerModeDetector: DeveloperModeDetector,
  private val onEvent: (SecurityMonitorEvent) -> Unit
) {
  companion object {
    private const val POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS = 4L
  }

  private val isRunning = AtomicBoolean(false)
  private var executor: ScheduledExecutorService? = null
  private var networkCallback: ConnectivityManager.NetworkCallback? = null
  private var screenshotObserver: ContentObserver? = null

  // Last known state, to only emit on rising edges.
  private var wasVpnConnected = false
  private var wasProxyEnabled = false
  private var wasRooted = false
  private var wasHookDetected = false
  private var wasDebuggerAttached = false
  private var wasTampered = false

  fun start() {
    if (!isRunning.compareAndSet(false, true)) return
    registerNetworkCallback()
    registerScreenshotObserver()
    startPolling()
  }

  fun stop() {
    if (!isRunning.compareAndSet(true, false)) return
    unregisterNetworkCallback()
    unregisterScreenshotObserver()
    executor?.shutdownNow()
    executor = null
  }

  private fun emit(type: String, message: String) {
    onEvent(SecurityMonitorEvent(type, message, System.currentTimeMillis().toDouble()))
  }

  // --- VPN / proxy (real push via ConnectivityManager) --------------------------------

  private fun registerNetworkCallback() {
    val cm = context.getSystemService(Context.CONNECTIVITY_SERVICE) as? ConnectivityManager ?: return
    val callback = object : ConnectivityManager.NetworkCallback() {
      override fun onCapabilitiesChanged(network: Network, capabilities: NetworkCapabilities) {
        val isVpn = capabilities.hasTransport(NetworkCapabilities.TRANSPORT_VPN)
        if (isVpn && !wasVpnConnected) {
          emit("VPN_CONNECTED", "VPN connection detected")
        }
        wasVpnConnected = isVpn

        val isProxy = networkMonitor.isProxyEnabled()
        if (isProxy && !wasProxyEnabled) {
          emit("PROXY_ENABLED", "System proxy configuration detected")
        }
        wasProxyEnabled = isProxy
      }
    }
    try {
      cm.registerNetworkCallback(NetworkRequest.Builder().build(), callback)
      networkCallback = callback
    } catch (_: Exception) {
      networkCallback = null
    }
  }

  private fun unregisterNetworkCallback() {
    val callback = networkCallback ?: return
    val cm = context.getSystemService(Context.CONNECTIVITY_SERVICE) as? ConnectivityManager
    try {
      cm?.unregisterNetworkCallback(callback)
    } catch (_: Exception) {
      // already unregistered / callback never took effect
    }
    networkCallback = null
  }

  // --- Screenshots (real push via MediaStore ContentObserver) -----------------------------

  private fun registerScreenshotObserver() {
    val observer = object : ContentObserver(Handler(Looper.getMainLooper())) {
      override fun onChange(selfChange: Boolean) {
        screenMonitor.markScreenshotDetected()
        emit("SCREENSHOT", "A screenshot was captured")
      }
    }
    try {
      context.contentResolver.registerContentObserver(
        MediaStore.Images.Media.EXTERNAL_CONTENT_URI,
        true,
        observer
      )
      screenshotObserver = observer
    } catch (_: Exception) {
      screenshotObserver = null
    }
  }

  private fun unregisterScreenshotObserver() {
    val observer = screenshotObserver ?: return
    try {
      context.contentResolver.unregisterContentObserver(observer)
    } catch (_: Exception) {
      // no-op
    }
    screenshotObserver = null
  }

  // --- Polled signals (no first-class Android push API) -------------------------------------

  private fun startPolling() {
    val scheduler = Executors.newSingleThreadScheduledExecutor()
    executor = scheduler
    scheduler.scheduleWithFixedDelay(
      { pollOnce() },
      POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS,
      POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS,
      TimeUnit.SECONDS
    )
  }

  private fun pollOnce() {
    try {
      val rooted = rootDetector.isRooted()
      if (rooted && !wasRooted) emit("ROOT_DETECTED", "Root access detected on this device")
      wasRooted = rooted

      val hooked = hookDetector.isAnyHookDetected()
      if (hooked && !wasHookDetected) emit("HOOK_DETECTED", "An instrumentation/hooking framework was detected")
      wasHookDetected = hooked

      val debugger = developerModeDetector.isDebuggerAttached()
      if (debugger && !wasDebuggerAttached) emit("DEBUGGER", "A debugger attached to the app process")
      wasDebuggerAttached = debugger

      val tampered = integrityChecker.isTampered()
      if (tampered && !wasTampered) emit("TAMPER", "App tamper signal detected")
      wasTampered = tampered
    } catch (_: Exception) {
      // A single failed poll cycle must never crash the monitoring loop.
    }
  }
}
```

`android/src/main/java/com/margelo/nitro/rsnativekit/security/util/ShellUtils.kt`:

```kt
package com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.util

import java.io.BufferedReader
import java.io.File
import java.io.InputStreamReader
import java.util.concurrent.TimeUnit

/**
 * Shared low-level helpers for the root/hook/emulator detectors. Every
 * process-exec call is bounded by a short timeout and never throws — a
 * detector that can't run a check should treat it as "not detected", not
 * crash the whole scan.
 */
internal object ShellUtils {
  /** Common locations where a `su`/`busybox`/etc. binary might live on a rooted device. */
  val COMMON_BIN_PATHS = arrayOf(
    "/data/local/",
    "/data/local/bin/",
    "/data/local/xbin/",
    "/sbin/",
    "/su/bin/",
    "/system/bin/",
    "/system/bin/.ext/",
    "/system/bin/failsafe/",
    "/system/sbin/",
    "/system/usr/we-need-root/",
    "/system/xbin/",
    "/cache/",
    "/data/",
    "/dev/"
  )

  /** True if `binaryName` exists (as a regular, readable file) in any of `COMMON_BIN_PATHS`. */
  fun binaryExistsOnCommonPaths(binaryName: String): Boolean {
    return COMMON_BIN_PATHS.any { dir -> File(dir, binaryName).exists() }
  }

  /** True if `binaryName` is resolvable on the process `PATH`. */
  fun binaryExistsOnPath(binaryName: String): Boolean {
    val path = System.getenv("PATH") ?: return false
    return path.split(File.pathSeparator).any { dir ->
      File(dir, binaryName).let { it.exists() && it.canExecute() }
    }
  }

  /**
   * Runs `command` with a hard [timeoutMs] deadline and returns combined
   * stdout, or `null` if the command failed, timed out, or couldn't start.
   */
  fun exec(command: Array<String>, timeoutMs: Long = 1500): String? {
    var process: Process? = null
    return try {
      process = ProcessBuilder(*command)
        .redirectErrorStream(true)
        .start()

      val finished = process.waitFor(timeoutMs, TimeUnit.MILLISECONDS)
      if (!finished) {
        process.destroyForcibly()
        return null
      }

      BufferedReader(InputStreamReader(process.inputStream)).use { it.readText() }
    } catch (_: Exception) {
      null
    } finally {
      process?.destroyForcibly()
    }
  }

  /** True if a given absolute file/directory path both exists and is writable. */
  fun isWritable(path: String): Boolean {
    return try {
      File(path).canWrite()
    } catch (_: Exception) {
      false
    }
  }

  /** Reads a whole file as text, or `null` on any failure (missing, unreadable, SELinux-denied, ...). */
  fun readFileOrNull(path: String): String? {
    return try {
      File(path).takeIf { it.isFile && it.canRead() }?.readText()
    } catch (_: Exception) {
      null
    }
  }
}

```

`android/src/test/java/com/margelo/nitro/rsnativekit/security/util/ShellUtilsTest.kt`:

```kt
package com.margelo.nitro.rsnativekit.security.util

import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
import org.junit.Assert.assertFalse
import org.junit.Assert.assertNull
import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
import org.junit.Test
import java.io.File

class ShellUtilsTest {

  @Test
  fun `binaryExistsOnCommonPaths returns false for a binary that does not exist`() {
    assertFalse(ShellUtils.binaryExistsOnCommonPaths("definitely-not-a-real-binary-12345"))
  }

  @Test
  fun `binaryExistsOnPath finds an executable that is actually on PATH`() {
    // `sh` is guaranteed to exist on every macOS/Linux CI runner this test executes on.
    assertTrue(ShellUtils.binaryExistsOnPath("sh") || ShellUtils.binaryExistsOnCommonPaths("sh"))
  }

  @Test
  fun `binaryExistsOnPath returns false for a made-up binary name`() {
    assertFalse(ShellUtils.binaryExistsOnPath("definitely-not-a-real-binary-12345"))
  }

  @Test
  fun `exec returns stdout for a simple command`() {
    val output = ShellUtils.exec(arrayOf("echo", "hello-from-shellutils"))
    assertEquals("hello-from-shellutils", output?.trim())
  }

  @Test
  fun `exec returns null for a command that does not exist`() {
    assertNull(ShellUtils.exec(arrayOf("definitely-not-a-real-command-12345")))
  }

  @Test
  fun `exec respects a short timeout instead of hanging forever`() {
    val output = ShellUtils.exec(arrayOf("sleep", "5"), timeoutMs = 100)
    assertNull(output)
  }

  @Test
  fun `isWritable reflects the real filesystem state`() {
    val tempDir = File(System.getProperty("java.io.tmpdir") ?: "/tmp")
    assertTrue(ShellUtils.isWritable(tempDir.path))
    assertFalse(ShellUtils.isWritable("/definitely/not/a/real/path/12345"))
  }

  @Test
  fun `readFileOrNull returns file contents when readable`() {
    val file = File.createTempFile("shellutils-test", ".txt")
    file.writeText("hello")
    try {
      assertEquals("hello", ShellUtils.readFileOrNull(file.path))
    } finally {
      file.delete()
    }
  }

  @Test
  fun `readFileOrNull returns null for a missing file`() {
    assertNull(ShellUtils.readFileOrNull("/definitely/not/a/real/file/12345"))
  }
}

```
